According to Girma Worke, the center's coordinator, the innovations were made by 12th grade students from the University of Gondar Community School with the help of the UoG Science and Technology Center. Automatic Hand wash and drier machine, locust trap system, and patient monitoring systems were developed by the students. The University of Gondar Research and Community Service Vice President Prof. Mersha Chanie and other relevant officials were present on the program. The innovations at the STEM Center will set a good example for the youth who have different interests and talents. This will allow them expand their horizons and inspire them to think big. #STEM #ScienceandTechnology #innovation #students #community #engineering #STEMeducation" "STEMpower gave an automatic hand sanitizer machine made in-house to FDRE Agency for Civil Society Organizations. The agency paid a visit at STEMpower Inc. Head office and was briefed on STEMpower’s activities in STEM Education through its STEM Centers established in nearly all Government Universities, High Schools, Education Bureaus and Science Academy. STEM Centers are specialized learning facilities that offer hands-on lab experience on Science, Technology, Engineering and Mathematics. STEMpower facilitated a visit for the Agency to its STEM Center and Science Museum found at Ethiopian Academy of Science and Addis Ababa Science & Technology University respectively aiming to give them more insight on its activities. #STEMpower #education #handsonlearning #STEM #STEMlabs #Innovation #STEMeducation #technology #africa #ethiopia" STEMpower - Addis Ababa Science & Technology University (Aastu_official) STEMpower - Addis Ababa Science & Technology University (Aastu_official) "Dambi Dollo University (DaDU) is one of the public higher Universities serving the country for more than 3 years with teaching, research, and community engagements in different academic disciplines. The University is committed to providing quality education at undergraduate and postgraduate levels and engaged in varieties of community service activities. STEMpower, DaDU Vice President for Research and Community service, and MoSHE have signed a Memorandum of Understanding for the full establishment of STEM Center at Dambi Dollo University to assist the development of Science and Technology in the area. Accordingly, the University had established Science, Technology, Engineering, and Mathematics (STEM) Centre under the office of Vice president for research and community service affairs in collaboration with STEMpower and the Ministry of Science & Higher Education (MoSHE) with the Motto “Inside every child is a scientist”. STEM Center is specialized learning facilities that offer hands-on experience to local-area where students voluntarily and eagerly enroll in various age-appropriate programs offered, at no tuition fee for the students. It is a site that provides quality professional development activities and resources to support STEM Education. Additionally, the center shall provide funds and technical help for students with innovative ideas, projects, talent hunting, and allowing them to use the center laboratories. Currently, the Center provides a weekend outreach program for top-ranked grade 12th students drawn from three respective preparatory schools at Kellem Secondary School to capacitate their potential to answer national examination questions by practicing and showing them some examples. DaDU Vice President for Research and Community Service Ass. Prof Yoseph Shiferraw and Academic vice president Dr. Buli Yohannis on their part gave a brief on the future plan of STEM Center on the launching day of the weekend outreach program, underscored that the complex will be influential in laying a scientific foundation to better community service. STEMpower provided DaDU STEM Center with Virtual Computer Lab and Electronics Lab equipment in the 1st and 2nd rounds respectively. The logistics were collected by Dambi Dollo University STEM Centre coordinator Dr.Faris Basha. These equipment are instrumental in creating a generation versed in scientific and technological capabilities. #STEMpower #DambiDolloUniversity #MoSHE #education #handsonlearning #STEM #STEMlabs #Innovation #STEMeducation #technology #ethiopia #africa" "Two STEMpower students at the Ethiopian Academy of Sciences explaining their project by using 3D Animation. " "STEMpower started the process to open the first ever STEM center in a prison, in this case Ethiopia’s Kality Federal Prison, at the southern edge of the capital city Addis Ababa. STEMpower is adding more educational options to the vocational school inside the Kality Prison, the goal being to contribute towards rehabilitation and reintegration of inmates after their release, particularly the younger prisoners. Deputy Commissioner General (FPA) Desta Asmelash Girmay had reached out to STEMpower, with the aim of expanding their mix of Kality Vocational School Prison options. At the request of the Deputy Commissioner, STEMpower started to install a Virtual Computing Lab and an Electronics Lab in the prison school. STEMpower has established a network of 42 Ethiopian STEM Centers, at almost every Ethiopian public university. It was thus natural to branch out to experimental high-potential STEM Center projects, e.g., the Commissioner’s unique vision. The STEM education movement is in high gear inside Ethiopia, so we plan for such situations. Our warehouse already contains the necessary lab equipment and furniture (i.e., lab tables, student seating, teacher desk and chair) for the Kality prison school." Thank you DHL for this wonderful support. #DHL #stempower #smartaid #students #teachers #empower #STEM #stemeducation #innovation #africa #ethiopia #impact #university #covid19 #health #technology #education" "STEMpowers’ South Sudan 🇸🇸 STORY On September 1st 2020, STEMpower celebrated the opening of its new STEM Center at Rumbek University Science Technology (""RUST"") in South Sudan. RUST-STEM is our 2nd STEM Center of 5 in South Sudan. Notably, RUST-STEM is also our first STEM Center in South Sudan that was installed entirely by our local team instead of our team of experts based in Addis Ababa (Ethiopia’s Capital 🇪🇹). Thus, we have made a giant first step towards fulfilling our dream of offering STEM Centers to benefit students throughout subSaharan countries. STEMpower (and its predecessor GFCT) had over the past decade already equipped and helped establish 43 STEM Centers in Ethiopia, plus 1 beachhead very large STEM Center in Juba University (South Sudan’s Capital 🇸🇸 ). Over the last year, we imagined that we might be able to extend our successful model to beyond Ethiopia and beyond Juba in South Sudan. With current additional centers being built in Rwanda 🇷🇼 , Somaliland, Tanzania 🇹🇿 and more. At the same time, our team (with our partner company SunGate Solar based in Wau, South Sudan) was successfully installing solar power 💡 at 3 more academic locations in the country. The spirit of cooperation in such a challenged country was apparent and significant. SunGate Solar president Mr Mou Riiny eagerly agreed to be our STEM Center visionary Cluster head of the planned STEM Centers across South Sudan. Juba U-STEM Director Mr Andrew Anda and Juba U Vice Chancellor graciously offered their help. Mr Richard Ring Ruach joined our band of change-makers. Simultaneously, our team based at STEMpower HQ in Addis Ababa had pivoted to consider how we could establish more STEM Centers beyond our ""comfort zone"". Therefore, we created videos, better documentation and training materials, as we were installing our most recent STEM Centers. Against all odds of the many challenges inherent in South Sudan, STEMpower put its plan into action. - Heavy rains on hundreds of miles of unpaved muddy roads DID NOT STOP US. 💪 - Importing tons of lab equipment into a remote landlocked nation DID NOT STOP US. 💪 - Uncertain political stability DID NOT STOP US. 💪 - Zero electric utility power in the entire country DID NOT STOP US, 💪 - and COVID19 DID NOT STOP US 💪 And here you can see the results. RUST Vice Chancellor Ms Pauline Riak ceremoniously opening RUST-STEM, our RUST-STEM Virtual Computer Lab and RUST-STEM Electronics Lab. 👨🏿‍💻 Stay tuned for more of STEMpower's pivots and evolutions, many of which are well underway. STEMpower is build a nation and continent wide STEM ecosystem across Africa that will enable its people to become world industry leaders 🌍 As believers in local know-how, localization, empowerment and self sustainability of Africa’s people we continue to work alongside them in their efforts and WE CALL our global partners to continue and join us in expanding the STEM ecosystem efforts across Africa alongside STEMpower. STEMpower #Education #STEM #Engineer #students #university #tech #impact #selfsustainable #TVET #school #entrepreneur #economy #Job #hub" "DESIGNING CIRCUIT BOARDS STEMpower team has now established 43 hands-on STEM Centers across Ethiopia enriching an entire generation of thousands of young engineers. The new generation of engineers have tested their electronics circuit designs with experimental arrangements, and now they are typically confident enough to commercialize their product ideas. STEMpower founder Mark Gelfand and his engineer son Evan realized that commercialization in most cases would require electronic components to be permanently and ruggedly secured. The technology is known as printed circuit boards. But commercialized circuit boards are custom to each application. Usually circuit boards can be expensive in small quantities, error-prone and risky in large-quantities. Plus, it's an environmental challenge to etch away the copper cladding while making large quantities. How to make circuit boards in Ethiopia? Gelfand brought the answer into our new FabLab. Four Voltera machines seemed to be exactly what was needed to produce modest quantities of commercial-quality custom-designed circuit boards. Allowing students to take their proven designs and develop miniaturized versions with far less time & financial risks, later enabling them to scale to high volumes of production. Quickly yet carefully, young engineers and our amazing STEM students, installed the Voltera equipment, and then studied its circuit board design software. Their first circuit board design was called ""Hello World"", which is a famous engineering phrase to quickly verify that basic systems are working. ""Hello World"" for our circuit boards meant blinking LEDs and beeps, plus an integrated circuit (IC), resistors and capacitors, and a 9 volt battery. For a faster jump towards commercialization, the team chose to utilize modern surface-mount technology, which involves advanced techniques such as parts placement and solder re-flow. It had been unimaginable that Ethiopian engineers would ever design their own circuits utilizing such beneficial technology. Today, the first commercially-viable high-quality custom circuit boards were manufactured, in Ethiopia, entirely by young Ethiopian engineers. #job #engineer #fablab #tech #africa #Ethiopia" Title THANK YOU Visa "STEMpower and MoE will host the Ethiopian Science and Engineering Fair 2020 on November 10, 2020, at Hyatt Regency. Students will show their showcase to attendees and there will also be a panel discussion on ""STEM Education and Innovation in Ethiopia and Africa - the past, the present and the future"". #ScienceandEngineeringFair #Innovations #STEMpower #MoE #STEMEducation " "Job Title: Finance and Administration Manager Job Description: Reporting to the CEO, the Finance and Admin Assistant leads all Administrative and accounting operations. US work perm - Required. General  This role requires outstanding professionalism and discretion, organization and attention to detail, energy and initiative, the ability to deftly multi-task and a willingness to work on a wide variety of projects and tasks.  Works independently on projects, from conception to completion, and must be able to work under pressure at times to handle a wide variety of activities and confidential matters with discretion.  Adhere to the organization’s code of ethics and maintain confidentiality at all times. Trustworthiness and sensitivity are essential qualities for this worker.  Develop mutual trust and build a courteous, respectful and compassionate team environment with other staff members, organizational leaders and clients.  Requires flexibility of schedule & teamwork. Administrative Support  Works closely and effectively with the CEO to keep her well informed of upcoming commitments and responsibilities, following up appropriately.  Prioritizes competing needs; handles matter expeditiously and proactively; and follows-through on projects to successful completion, often with deadline pressures.  Perform general administrative duties and take on additional tasks as directed by the President & CEO and the Country Director. Finance:  Responsible for Financial Performance and Viability for the fiscal integrity of STEMpower, to include submission to the Board of a proposed annual budget and quarterly financial statements, which accurately reflect the financial condition of the organization.  Responsible for providing for basic bookkeeping tasks, running financial reports, assisting with annual audit etc.  Maintain control of the following areas: general ledger, accounts payable, expense reports, billing, and payroll.  Administer US-based payroll, using various tools and partnering with ADP payroll company to deliver flawless payments.  Assist in issuing timely, accurate, and complete financial statements for Board of Directors and the Chief Executive Director.  Ensure that all financial transactions are processed accurately, in a timely manner, and in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles.  Provide useful financial insights to help make better decisions about formulating and executing strategy and provide guidance and analysis to executive and operational management to improve results. Report and Record Keeping  Preparing presentations, reports, social media, newsletters and other special mailings.  Maintain a filing system for receipts and invoices for monetary grants, and other financial transaction.  Maintaining filing system for all activities of STEMpower including agreements, purchase order, custom clearance and deliverance, launching STEM centers in soft and hard copy in collaboration with country offices.  Create an online tracking system to update project status and related works. Qualifications: • Bachelor’s degree in accounting or finance. CPA is preferred but not required. • Minimum of 5 years of finance and accounting experience with international non-governmental organizations. • Solid experience in coordinating audit activities and managing reporting, budget development and analysis, accounts payable and receivable, general ledger, payroll, and accounting for investments. • Nonprofit experience is a must. • Strategic thinker who possesses solid business acumen and is able to organize and manage multiple priorities. • Exceptional verbal and written communications skills and the ability to interact effectively with others, both internally and externally. • Excellent time management skills, with the ability to perform multiple tasks and meet critical deadlines while maintaining accuracy and quality. • Proven ability to make complex and time-sensitive decisions in the best interests of the organization While the above is a description of the essential functions of the position, other duties may be assigned. This job description is subject to change at the discretion of management. The position is based in Boston, MA or/and online. STEMpower Inc. is an equal opportunity employer. Please visit our website at www.STEMpower.org to learn more. To apply, please submit a cover letter and resume to info@stempower.org; Kidist@stempower.org; and kidogg@gmail.com with Finance/Admin assistant in the subject line. The position is available for immediate hire, with applications considered on a rolling basis. Candidates are encouraged to submit applications as early as possible." "Living in places like South Sudan, means no access to electricity (or perhaps few hours a day usage of a costly noisy diesel generator means there’s no internet, no access to proper education (let alone engineering education to build their country), a lessened quality of life, and a life that radically worsens at dusk. STEMpower, with its partners SmartAID, @DHL and the incredible team from Sungate Solar Solution have installed our 3rd solar power in the town of Torit, three hours drive east of South Sudan's capital city Juba. That green generating system powers our our 3rd STEM Center (containing a high-efficiency Virtual Computer Lab, Electronics Lab, and 3D printer) is now our 3rd operating STEM Center in SouthSudan. Moreover, STEMpower's newest STEM Center is part of SouthSudan's ambitious TVET (Technical, Vocational and Entrepreneurial Training) Centers movement. "STEMpower team led by the Country Representative, Kidist Gebreamlak, along with the Federal Prison Commission- Ethiopia Commissioner General Damene Darota, inaugurated the STEM Center established at Kality Correctional facility, commonly known as Abba Samuel, on May 11th, 2021 in Addis Ababa, Ethiopia. Speaking at the graduation ceremony, Ms. Kidist said that STEMpower's vision is to bring economic impact and solve the problems of society with the help of science and technology. STEMpower is laying the groundwork in Science, Technology, Engineering, and Mathematics education (STEM), she said. Speaking at the graduation ceremony, Commissioner General Damene Darota said, the main mission of the STEM Center at the correctional facility is to equip inmates with the knowledge and hands-on skills in Electronics and Information Technology. ""We are working closely to meet this mission,"" he said. Commissioner General Damene Darota called on STEMpower and Akaki Kaliti Sub-City Education Bureau to continue their efforts to create manpower that can keep pace with the technology and equip the facility with modern gadgets in the future. He said this STEM Center, which is built by STEMpower, will greatly support the education being given at the correctional facility. The machine consists of a 2.5 Watt LASER diode, three stepper motors for the x and y axis motions (two for the x-axis and one for the y-axis), an Arduino Uno control board, stepper motor drivers and a 12 volt power supply unit. The machine is capable of performing engraving and profiling works on materials like wood, leather, plastics and acrylic plates. The development of this Robotic Arm is one of FabLab’s in-house projects. The person behind this work is our enthusiastic Electro-Mechanical Engineer Israel Gebru. He has been working on the design and development of the Robotic Arm for nearly 2 months. The in-house Robotic Arm project serves as a teaching aid for our Robotics curriculum for secondary and University students. The Robotic Arm project includes Micro-controller and 5 servo motors. Motion sensors make sure the arm moves to the correct position. The robot's custom printed circuit boards were manufactured in our FabLab. We 3D-printed most of the plastic parts, including the gears. With this Robotic Arm, we hope to develop the capacity of African students and expand the field of robotics, which will eventually be employed to boost productivity in Ethiopia's industrial sector. STEMpower, A place of great Innovation! Please support, www.STEMpower.org/donate, our endeavors and expansion work in Sub-Saharan African countries. #Inside_Every_Child_Is_A_Scientist #STEMpower #Innovation #Technology #EastAfrica #Robotics #FabLab #Roboticarm #Robot #Math #Engineering #Design #STEMeducation #STEMforall #Inspiration #RobotARmProject #EduactionalRobot #Ethiopia #Sub_Saharan_Africa" "A CNC (Computer Numerical Control) milling machine has been developed at STEMpower Fabrication laboratory (Fab-Lab), located in Addis Ababa, in the premises of the Ministry of Education of Ethiopia. The development of this CNC machine is part of FabLab's in-house projects. The person behind this work is one of our diligent technical Engineers, Merkineh Petros, who has been working on the design and development of the machine for nearly 3 months. The in-house machine project, the ""CNC Router Machine"", is capable of cutting and engraving materials such as foam board and MDF wood fiberboard. Merkineh's CNC machine utilizes one spindle motor to cut and engrave the material, plus three stepper motors to move the spindle motor along three axes (X, Y, and Z). The design of the cutting and engraving plan is done on a standard PC computer and electronically sent to the CNC machine as G-code (the world's most popular language for instructing machine tool movement). STEMpower recommends that Merkineh's design form the basis for the installation of sturdy Ethiopian CNC machines to eventually be installed in each of Ethiopia's 3000 government high schools. STEMpower believes that the development of such modern and cutting-edge machines will contribute to import substitution and simultaneously inspire youths to say it is possible to build machines locally. We are amid establishing in partnership with smartAID and DHL, DHL Africa another 30 more STEM Centers across Sub-Saharan Africa, bringing our total to 80 operating STEM Centers by end of 2022, and planning for more than 100 by end of 2023. Our installation teams operate out of Ethiopia, South Sudan, and Rwanda, traveling to all countries throughout Africa. "ETHIOPIA: The STEMpower-VISA Entrepreneurship Incubation Program’s third annual pitching competition final took place on March 24-25, at the STEMpower FabLab in the Ethiopian Ministry of Education headquarters in Addis Ababa. In this year's finals, student teams creating the top 15 business ideas were pitching their business proposals, which spanned agriculture, engineering, technology, and service industries. Each year, our Entrepreneurship Incubation Program runs for 3 months. This year, 1600 student trainees initially organized themselves into hundreds of teams, each team proposing a business idea. Those students subsequently underwent intensive training and coaching by STEMpower staff and volunteer market-specific experts. Rounds of judging narrowed the field into the top 15 teams, for the 2022 competition finals. The trainees agreed that the process helped them turn their innovative ideas into a business, become financially literate, network with like-minded people. A student trainee involved two projects, Genet Mulugeta, described her experience as exciting and educational. ""I applied for this program because I had previously competed in an innovation incubation competition at my university (Adama Science & Technology University abbreviated 'ASTU'). My project there was judged in the top 6. When I later came across the STEMpower Entrepreneurial Incubation Program, I felt I was in the right place, even after the first training session. I was always eager to get back for the next sessions.” Another student trainee, soon-to-be mechanical engineer Genet R, said, ""There might not be a job when you go out there, but there are a lot of problems that you can address."" He urged other student trainees to keep an entrepreneurial mind set."" Another student trainee, Samuel Girma, who attended the ASTU STEM Center while in high school, together with his sister Haymanot Girma, invented an innovative school bell, which could be used at Ethiopia's 10,000 schools. He said, ""The consistent follow ups of the STEMpower Entrepreneurship Program have pushed us to keep going, we have improved our project and become organized"". "Happy International Day of Mathematics! “Mathematics is the way the universe speaks”, says Biruk Demissie, a STEMpower curriculum developer, focused primarily on Biomechanics, a subject infused with mathematics. STEMpower is taking the initiative to establish the first-ever Mathematics Laboratory in Ethiopia, where students will explore patterns and mathematical ideas from elementary to advanced levels."" Biruk Demissie is leading the STEMpower Math-Lab project. Topics draw from Algebra, Geometry, and the core ideas of Probability, Statistics, and Calculus. STEMpower and Addis PCB Design LLC have agreed to provide technical training for Ethiopian youth and expand STEM education in the country, as well as the Fab-lab operations, by raising funds and purchasing important machineries such as pick and place machines. Training on digital hardware implementation was conducted from June 9–17, 2022 at the AAiT STEM center. The seven-day training was focused on the practical aspects of converting a digital design idea into a working prototype using free software. Trainees implemented different types of test benches and simulations on FPGA boards. FPGA stands for Field Programmable Gate Array. It is a digital programmable chip that is used in electronic products and offers re-programmability capability. This semiconductor chip is based on programmable interconnection circuits and logic blocks that can be modified to suit the needs of the user. FPGA is mostly used for high data rate applications such as video & image processing, telecom & datacom, and defense & space. The benefit of this program is for future employment, building on the practical, hands-on principles the students learn in our STEM labs and expanding further in our Fab Labs. Twenty-five students received certificates at the AAiT closing ceremony after completing the training. Minister of Innovation and Technology, H.E. Dr. Belete Molla, and H.E. Mr. Solomon Soka, Deputy Director-General of INSA, both spoke during the ceremony, expressing their support for the initiative and the students' efforts. Visits to Fab-lab's in-house projects were made by all of the guests. We would like to express our sincere gratitude for the program's success to Mr. Gobena Merga, CEO of ATG, Dr. Eyosias Yoseph, from ATG, and Ms. Ruhama Markos, Managing Director of Addis PCB. Future operations of the collaboration will focus on sustaining the programs of practical STEM education training as both parties will be working closely in the sector. The project hopes to decrease the damaging effects and commercial challenges caused by the annual rains. Among the damage is that STEMpower must postpone the completion of its upcoming STEM Centers until at least February 2023, when we expect the roads (unpaved throughout the nation) will become dry enough to support trucking and other logistics. Our idea is to pump away excess floodwaters, using trees that we will plant alongside the muddy sections of roadways. Initially, we will use local trees, but we think careful & responsible imported trees might eventually provide a more efficient pumping capacity. The project is being coordinated with South Sudan's Lakes region Ministry of Agriculture and the forestry education center. The STEM perspective includes the calculation of the volume of water sucked into a tree's roots, the amount of water evaporated away from the tree leaves, and the amount of strengthening of the roadway surface due to buried tree roots. A side study will be the reforestation of parks in the capital city of Juba. We'll plant shade trees that might beautify the city, encourage social engagement, and provide shade from the glaring sun overhead. The course is titled ""Chemical Micro factory"" because of the inclusion of continuous (industrial) processes, which supplement typical learning by batch process chemistry. 18 female and 13 male high-school students eagerly learned the novel engineering discipline. Their practical curricula included the production of soap, paper production from plant stalk, paper recycling, biodiesel fuel, and oil from seeds -- all by both batch and continuous modes of production, and all by drawing from locally-available input materials. STEMpower's STEM enrichment programs in Sub-Saharan Africa go beyond even developed nations' STEM enrichment programs. Moreover, the BDU teacher-mentors themselves participated in STEMpower's ""Train the Trainers programs, rapidly spreading useful knowledge throughout the nation(s). The ""Chemical Micro factory"" curriculum is the product of a collaboration between Case University (""CWRU"") in Cleveland Ohio (USA), Addis Ababa Institute of Technology (""AAIT"") in Ethiopia, and STEMpower in the USA & Africa. STEMpower is a non-profit organization working in STEM (Science, Technology, Engineering, and Math) education across many Sub-Saharan African countries. Our team has been supporting the education sector for more than a decade by establishing and supporting STEM centers for students in secondary education, together with the relevant government bodies in the sector. Students of the Center showcased Smart solutions for agriculture, climate change, and health at the 112th Zimbabwe Agricultural Show. They demonstrated how to fly a drone and explained its application in agriculture, climate change, and health. In agriculture, farmers are using drones to disperse pesticides and fertilizers. The results include reduced operation costs, improved crop quality, and yield rate. In climate change, drone technology is already being used in innovative ways around the world and are directly combating climate change by providing cheaper and more effective ways to document, monitor, and prevent deforestation and greenhouse gas emission. In health, Drones could deliver medications and supplies to patients being cared for in the home instead of in a hospital-based setting. Blood samples collected at home are being delivered to the lab by a drone. Over 80% of WUA's multi-disciplinary students are female, aiming to fill the gap in gender equality across Zimbabwe. The Center has Smart Class and Electronics labs that benefit thousands of high school students in the university's catchment area. Today, the lab is visiting the Dibayu primary school. 58 upper primary students (girls and boys in grades 7-8) are learning about electronics and computer controlled systems. Prior to our Mobile Lab, there has been no practical way that these students would otherwise have a chance to experience learning such subjects in rural areas. The lab was designed and built by a collaboration of STEMpower and local industrial factories (IMG and Porta, both of which also donate a portion of their profits to STEMpower Ethiopia). The bare chassis of a heavy-duty Isuzu truck was fitted with a custom cargo box, its interior improved with electrical wiring, lighting, ventilation, desks, and stairs. Everything is in place to build a fleet of Mobile Labs across Sub-Saharan Africa, as well as to swap in more forms of engineering and sciences. Check our website: www.STEMpower.org. #Inside_Every_Child_Is_A_Scientist #STEMpower #STEMpower_FabLab #Ethiopia #Sub_Saharan_Africa #Innovation #Engineering #Technology #Machine_Design_and_Manufacturing" "Ethiopia 🇪🇹 STEMpower's unique FabLab facility bridges the transition from hands-on STEM education to product manufacturing. Today, our technical expert Israel Gebru publicly showed his in-house creation, ""Automatic Silk Screen Printer"". The machine prints full-color texts and images on various material input items, e.g. papers, fabrics, plastics, and metal plates. Items can be up to 20 cm thick, and the silk screen printing rate is 12 items printed per minute. The design and development of the machine took four months. The Mechanical, Electrical, and Software tasks were performed from scratch by our talented FabLab engineer. The tasks include designing, machining, welding, fastening, circuit board printing, 3D printing, writing code for the Arduino Mega microcontroller, assembling, and testing. In addition to the machine's quality and speed, the machine also promotes a safe operating environment. Its sensors trigger an alarm sound if someone enters a restricted area near the machine. Israel Gebru remarked, ""Our machine is fully-functional, industrial-grade, durable, reliable, and ready for mass production. I learned much during the process and now gain the knowledge and skill to build more sophisticated machines that can substitute imports."" Breaking free of importation is a national goal of this emerging country. Achieving that goal is the purpose of the STEMpower endeavor, whose 73 STEM Centers now span Sub-Saharan Africa, from ocean to ocean. Whereas STEMpower's innovative FabLabs, mobile STEM labs, and Entrepreneurship programs are first proven in Ethiopia. Some metal-cutting laser services were donated by long-time STEMpower supporter IMG Metal, in Dukem town. Would you like to sponsor STEMpower innovation? Check our website: www.STEMpower.org #STEMpower #School #Student #GirlsInSTEM #STEMforAll #Ethiopia #Education #Innovation #IoT #BasicElectronics # #CADModeling #Fasebook #BringEvryoneForward" "Ethiopia 🇪🇹 STEMpower announces the completion of its 6th solar-powered system in Ethiopia, powering the STEM Center at the Ethiopian Academy of Sciences (EAS) which was installed in partnership with DHL and smartAID, located in Addis Ababa. The integration of solar energy with the main electric utility grid system ensures a consistent reliable electricity supply to the EAS STEM Center, regardless of any rolling blackouts or loss of hydropower that can occur during seasonal droughts. Ethiopia's national electric grid system is almost entirely powered by water power, taking advantage of the nation's mountainous terrain. STEMpower's other five solar-powered Ethiopian STEM Centers are located in Foka (Bishoftu), Hawassa, Semera, BahirDar, and Gondar. Foka also serves as STEMpower's solar install training center. All of the solar-equipped Ethiopian STEM Centers generate a continuous 8 kW during the operating hours of the STEM Centers. Our 19 STEM Centers in nearby South Sudan are entirely dependent on solar power, as there is no electric utility grid in that country. "South Sudan 🇸🇸 STEMpower South Sudan has launched a tree-planting initiative in the Ruweng-Pareng Administrative Zone to mitigate perennial flooding. The semi-arid treeless area has been severely damaged by flash floods and thunderstorms, causing muddy roadways that challenge all activities for months. STEMpower alone has funded the nation's multi-locale tree initiative projects, with no contribution by any other donor. As a bonus, the first phases of the initiative have already brought sunshade relief and beautiful parks to the residents of Juba and Wau cities. The Ruwang initiative ceremony was celebrated by government representatives, local leaders, and STEMpower board members. The training was given by STEMpower's Fab-Lab Mechanical Engineer Biruk Demissie and Biomedical Engineer Aynalem Derese. The labs use an interdisciplinary biomechanics curriculum designed by Biruk Demissie. It covers topics such as the fundamentals of movement, anatomy, anthropometry, kinematics, kinetics, human joint biomechanics, musculoskeletal biomechanics, and biofluid mechanics. Grades 9–12 students took the hands-on training about force measurement, fluid viscosity assessment, and material stress–strain analysis. Kaleab Yimer, a student trainee, said, ""I used to focus only on software and physics, but learning about biomechanics gave me insight into the medical field in general and anatomy in particular."" www.STEMpower.org #STEMpower #STEM #Africa #Ethiopia #biomechanics #STEMeducation" "Rwanda 🇷🇼 STEMpower's Gitwe Adventist College STEM Center in southern Rwanda is currently providing Internet of Things (IoT) training to a total of 33 students. " "Ethiopia 🇪🇹 STEMpower Ethiopia adds factory automation to its wide array of engineering topics. STEMpower Ethiopia's flagship Foka STEM Center in Bishoftu City serves as the National Training Center for STEM Education in Ethiopia. United Kingdom-based Bedford College has trusted STEMpower to spread hands-on Industrial Automation knowledge across Ethiopia. The heart of modern automation is a rugged Computerized device known as Programmable Logical Controllers (""PLC""), typically used on a factory floor. Bedford College donated various PLC and associated devices to the STEMpower-hosted training program. During the week-long training, Bedford College visiting lecturer John Paintin covered PLC types, interfaces, programming languages, and sample automation projects. At the certification ceremony upon successful program completion, Dr. Simenew Keskes, STEMpower Ethiopia's Country Director, explained ""STEMpower follows the model of Training the Trainers to spread deep impact throughout its vast array of STEM Centers established across the nation and beyond"". Expert trainers from the STEM Centers at Addis Ababa Institute of Technology, Ethiopian Academy of Sciences, Adama Science & Technology University, Oromia Development Association, Arba Minch University, Kallamino Boarding School, and Foka, all attended the week-long program. ODA Special Boarding School trainer Mekonnen Teka said, ""The PLC training inspired me to develop innovative solutions for real-world challenges."" www.STEMpower.org #STEMpower #STEM #Engineering #Ethiopia #Africa #PLC #Industrial #Automation #PLC" "South Sudan 🇸🇸 Yesterday at the University of Juba STEM Center Robotics Lab, students from Darling Wisdom and Don Bosco secondary schools came to learn about robotics. The first group delved into creating digital circuits using real electronic components known as ""integrated circuits."" Armed with voltmeters, students deciphered the binary logic states (""1"" and ""0"") within their constructed circuits. Meanwhile, the second group engaged in the analysis of circuitry over time, employing oscilloscopes. This initiative, replicated across 109 STEM Centers in sub-Saharan Africa, consistently empowers thousands of students to grapple with complex concepts, preparing them for the demands of modern-day careers." "Uganda 🇺🇬 Last week, in southeastern Uganda, STEMpower's STEM Center at Busitema University established in partnership with DHL and smartAID gave 26 students a double dose of hands-on experience with Electrical Engineering. The first dose taught students how to create digital circuits using real electronic components called ""integrated circuits"". Students used voltmeters to read the states (basically the ""1"" and ""0"" of binary logic) within the circuitry they built. The states are either static (i.e., not changing over time) or deliberating changing sharply from ""1"" to ""0"" and vice-versa. The second dose taught students to analyze circuitry over time, using oscilloscopes. The oscilloscope display is like a graph, where the X axis represents time, from left to right, and the vertical Y axis represents the voltage or digital state ""1"" or ""0"". Amazingly, oscilloscopes can display a very slow change in the digital state up to 10 seconds across, as well as a fast change (up to 20 million repeating changes per second) in the digital state. 109 STEMpower STEM Centers across sub-Saharan Africa continually offer thousands of students each engage with difficult concepts, a requirement of modern-day careers. Please consider supporting STEMpower to reach every deserving student-engineer in this neglected major part of Africa. www.STEMpower.org #STEMpower #STEMeducation #BusitemaUniversity #Uganda #Africa #Education #STEM" "Kenya 🇰🇪 STEMpower's STEM Center at Beverly School of Kenya {""BSK"") has created a hands-on Robotics Training Program for its middle and senior school students. Local expert Kuot Achuil trained university staff on proper tree planting techniques. Most of the trees arrived as small saplings created by a local nursery. The first experiment was on titration to analyze the acidity of different samples to determine the concentration of an unknown solution by reacting it with a known solution, and the other one was a saponification practical which involved the reaction of fats or oils with a strong base to produce soap and glycerol. The students learned about the properties and applications of soap, as well as the environmental and health benefits of using natural and biodegradable products. The practical also demonstrated the interdisciplinary nature of chemistry and biology, as the students analyzed the molecular structures and functions of the reactants and products. The students expressed their satisfaction and interest in the practical, which enhanced their understanding and skills in both fields. These practical activities were part of the STEMpower program, which aims to support STEM education in Sub-Saharan Africa by providing high-quality equipment, materials, and training to local schools and teachers. A few years ago, Ethiopian federal law was reformed to allow a local charity to operate a business to generate revenue in support of its charitable operations. This is the story of STEMpower Ethiopia's manufacturing journey. Five years ago, we created a Fab Lab shop facility for young inventors, so that they would have free access to manufacturing tools capable of prototyping useful products. From that base, our electro-mechanical expert, Israel Gebru, and his Fab Lab team have used those tools to develop ever more sophisticated powerful machines, increasing our manufacturing capacity until we outgrew our showcase floor at the Ministry of Education headquarters (, and moved the Fab Lab to our own industrial building). An example of our Fab Lab's products is a high-speed silkscreen printer, from which we decorate high-quality promotional t-shirts for the youth who attend our many programs around the country. We also discovered that our precision silkscreen printer could serve in the manufacture of custom electronic circuit boards. Meanwhile, we became aware that nearly all of Ethiopia's 10,000 public schools did not use any classroom time management system -- instead, a school administrator would look at their watch to determine when to ring the school bell. STEMpower Ethiopia set out to design a better system for all the schools in Ethiopia. The attached photos document some of the many steps of manufacturing our ""Automatic Bell System"". Nearly every step is our own innovation. STEMpower donated ""Automatic Bell System"" serial# 000001 to the National Educational Materials Production and Distribution Enterprise (""EMPDE""). ""STEMpower' time-management system has made our own work much easier,"" says Dagnachew Engida, a gatekeeper at EMPDE. Co-gatekeeper Birkinesh Shume says ""This is amazing! It saves time and effort. Thanks!"" Our first batch of 50 systems is designed to catch any glitches, after which we will further improve our manufacturing capacity with ever more sophisticated development. Implementing innovation now. Kalamino Special High School is located in the southern part of Mekelle, the capital city of Ethiopia's northern Tigray region. Kalamino Special High School is the home of Ethiopia's 2nd STEM Center, founded by STEMpower's management team 13 years ago. The school STEM equipment survived the Ethiopia-Tigray conflict largely because the lab equipment was wisely packed into boxes that were safely placed in a storage room. Much of the equipment thus survived the conflict, and the STEM Center has resumed its intended purpose of educating Students. Also surviving that conflict is the Mekelle University Science Museum, located in downtown Mekelle. STEMpower's management team hadcreated that Science Museum 13 years ago, using interactive displays manufactured by MadaTech Museum in Haifa, Israel. Kalamino STEM Center director Daniel Tesfay often brings his Kalamino students to visit the Science Museum. He writes, ""I share information about STEMpower projects with my students. We frequently visit one of your most impressive works, the Mekelle University Science Museum. During our visits, the students are excited and amazed by what they see. The exhibits and hands-on experiences leave a lasting impression on them, sparking their curiosity and enthusiasm for science and innovation."" #STEMpower #Ethiopia #Africa #STEMeducation #STEM" "Burundi 🇧🇮 The ""STEMHub Burundi"" project, powered by STEMpower in partnership with the Israel in Rwanda, has launched accelerated training sessions at the University of Burundi's Kamenge Campus. The initiative equips Burundian youth with essential digital skills for the job market. As a young and densely-populated country, Burundi faces challenges in youth education and employment. Nearly every student had lacked access to practical hands-on STEM learning. STEMpower overcomes those challenges, by providing students with the tools they need. For example, last week, led by University of Burundi trainer Ciza Innocent, 32 visiting students from New School and SOS School learned online research, image handling, document formatting, and other technical skills for professional success. High school student Prencia Ange Korineza said, ""The hands-on STEM skills I'm gaining here will be critical when I finish school and enter the job market, especially because I'm pursuing computer science."" The country's two STEM Centers are busy every day. STEMpower continues to expand its family of STEM Centers while providing hundreds of thousands of students with access to transformative STEM education. As of this writing, we've installed 140 STEM Centers across 33 countries, thus well along our goal to reach students in all 43 countries that comprise Sub-Saharan Africa.
